  - `data/raw/imagery/night2012.ppm` (local, not committed)
- Network: no; deterministic: yes; kind: derive; after: nothing

#### 42. `build-aerial-intensity2020` (tools/build-aerial.py)

- Command: `python3 cwplans/tools/build-aerial.py intensity2020`
- Method: Mosaics the 2020 LiDAR intensity tiles over the model box at 3 m a pixel, stretches them and saves a grey JPEG.
- Rules:
  - Negative values are set to 0.
  - Stretch between the 1st and 99th percentile of the non-zero pixels (on a 0-4096 scale), then gamma 0.6.
- Inputs:
  - **ea-dsm-history** (Environment Agency LiDAR DSM survey tiles 1999 to 2022, OGL v3.0): https://environment.data.gov.uk/tiles/collections/survey/national_lidar_programme_intensity/2020/1/<tile>; tiles TQ3075, TQ3080, TQ3575, TQ3580 (GeoTIFF with TFW in ZIP) [manual download]
  - `data/raw/imagery/national_lidar_programme_intensity-2020-1-*.zip` (local, not committed)
  - [docklands/data/tex/textures.json](docklands/data/tex/textures.json)
- Outputs:
  - [docklands/data/tex/intensity2020.jpg](docklands/data/tex/intensity2020.jpg)
  - [docklands/data/tex/textures.json](docklands/data/tex/textures.json)
  - `data/raw/imagery/intensity2020.ppm` (local, not committed)
- Network: no; deterministic: yes; kind: derive; after: nothing

#### 59. `check-fp16-shaders` (tools/check-fp16-shaders.mjs)

- Command: `node cwplans/tools/check-fp16-shaders.mjs [--no-browser]`
- Method: Checks the 3D page's shaders for what a phone GPU would do differently from the headless SwiftShader renderer: opens the page in headless Chromium, captures every compiled shader source, and counts each program's uniform rows, varyings, samplers and attributes against the WebGL 1 minimums; then re-runs the shaders' risky maths (hashes, time arguments, far positions, derivatives) in Node with every intermediate rounded to IEEE binary16, beside fp32.
- Rules:
  - fp16: round to nearest, ties to even, 10-bit mantissa, exponents -14 to 15 with subnormals, overflow to infinity (Node 22 has no Math.f16round).
  - A case fails only for a shader declared mediump (on a phone mediump may be fp16); cases for shaders that ask for highp where present (HIP, PRE) are reported as notes, because every OpenGL ES 3 GPU has highp in fragment shaders.
  - Each case quotes the shader text it mirrors; when that text is gone from the page the case is reported STALE and the tool fails.
  - Uniform rows are counted without packing (every float, vec2 and vec3 a whole row), so a real count is at most the one shown; minimums: 128 vertex and 16 fragment uniform vectors, 8 varyings, 8 texture units, 8 attributes.
  - Writes nothing; exit 1 on a failing or stale case.
- Inputs:
  - [docklands/index.html](docklands/index.html)
  - [docklands/sky.js](docklands/sky.js)
  - [docklands/line-styles.js](docklands/line-styles.js)
- Outputs: none (prints only)
- Network: no; deterministic: yes; kind: check; after: nothing
- Faults: 

#### 60. `check-line-styles` (tools/check-line-styles.mjs)

- Command: `node cwplans/tools/check-line-styles.mjs [--out DIR] [--style lines|vectrex]`
- Method: Opens the 3D page with the Line drawing style in headless Chromium at four views and sizes, renders the line drawing and makes the Plotter SVG of the same camera, draws the SVG at the canvas size in the plotter's own page frame and matches the ink pixel by pixel; prints the edge counts, buffer bytes, build time, edges drawn, frame time and recall and precision within 1 and 2 px.
- Rules:
  - Ink: luma under 0.8 in the line drawing; the SVG's strokes drawn black at one canvas pixel, the credit left out.
  - Recall: plot ink with line-drawing ink within the tolerance (a square of 2 x tol + 1 px); precision: the other way.
  - Fails on a page error, a style that did not switch on, or recall under 95% or precision under 97% at 2 px.
  - Frame times come from SwiftShader (the GPU in software): not a phone. Writes images only with --out.
- Inputs:
  - [docklands/index.html](docklands/index.html)
  - [docklands/line-styles.js](docklands/line-styles.js)
  - [docklands/plotter-svg.js](docklands/plotter-svg.js)
- Outputs: none (prints only)
- Network: no; deterministic: no; kind: check; after: nothing
- Faults: 

#### Hand step `page-assets`: Pixel-art palette and music list for the 3D page

- By: hand; date: 2026-10-03
- Method: The pixel-art palette: 24 colours extracted from an owner-supplied reference picture by PIL median cut and k-means (a one-off run; the picture is not stored), plus 9 accent colours added by hand. The music list: tracks chosen by hand from Wikimedia Commons with title, artist, licence and page.
- Rules:
  - Pixel-art colours do not follow height alone (owner, 2026-10-03: it reads as a heat map); materials carry the variety.
  - Music: CC BY 3.0 tracks (Kevin MacLeod) served by Wikimedia Commons with Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *, so the page can analyse them; the credit is shown while a track plays.
- Inputs:
  - **owner-supplied** (supplied by the repository owner, owner's)
  - **commons-music** (Music by Kevin MacLeod, CC BY 3.0 (attribution; not share-alike))
- Outputs:
  - [docklands/data/pixel-palette.json](docklands/data/pixel-palette.json)
  - [docklands/data/music.json](docklands/data/music.json)
- Decisions recorded in: docklands/data/pixel-palette.json; docklands/data/music.json
- Not committed as code: The palette extraction was a one-off script and is not committed.

### 17. Facades

#### 45. `facade-measure` (registry/sources/facades/tools/facade.py)

- Command: `cd cwplans/registry/sources/facades/work && python3 ../tools/facade.py <img> <out_prefix> x0 y0 x1 y1`
- Method: Rectifies a hand-chosen facade region of an open photo and measures its bay and floor periods and its colours.
- Rules:
  - OpenCV LSD segments; RANSAC vertical and horizontal vanishing points; affine rectification by homography.
  - Periods by autocorrelation of gradient profiles, FFT cross-check; the fundamental chosen by looking at the patch.
  - Colours: k-means, k = 3, in Lab.
- Inputs:
  - `data/raw/facades/<cwb-id>/src-<id>.jpg` (local, not committed)
  - [registry/sources/facades/work/rois.json](registry/sources/facades/work/rois.json)
- Outputs:
  - [registry/sources/facades/work/meas.json](registry/sources/facades/work/meas.json)
- Network: no; deterministic: yes; kind: derive; after: nothing
- Hand judgement: The facade region in each photo is chosen by eye (registry/sources/facades/work/rois.json); the fundamental period is chosen by looking at the patch; the outputs are collected into meas.json by hand.
- Note: Also tools/measure.py for a patch cut by hand. The outputs were collected into meas.json by hand.

#### 46. `facade-measure-patch` (registry/sources/facades/tools/measure.py)

- Command: `cd cwplans/registry/sources/facades/work && python3 ../tools/measure.py <img> x0 y0 x1 y1 <out.jpg> [k]`
- Method: Periods and colours of a patch cut by hand from a photo, with no rectification (for faces seen square on).
- Rules:
  - Same measure and colour functions as facade.py.
- Inputs:
  - `data/raw/facades/<cwb-id>/src-<id>.jpg` (local, not committed)
- Outputs:
  - [registry/sources/facades/work/meas.json](registry/sources/facades/work/meas.json)
- Network: no; deterministic: yes; kind: derive; after: nothing
- Hand judgement: The patch is cut by hand; the outputs are collected into meas.json by hand.
- Note: Outputs collected into meas.json by hand.

#### 47. `facade-metric` (registry/sources/facades/tools/metric.py)

- Command: `cd cwplans/registry/sources/facades/work && python3 ../tools/metric.py`
- Method: Metric aspect of each rectified patch from its vanishing points and a focal length.
- Rules:
  - Focal length from the two vanishing points when plausible, else a 26 mm-equivalent phone lens.
- Inputs:
  - `data/raw/facades/<cwb-id>/src-<id>.jpg` (local, not committed)
  - [registry/sources/facades/work/rois.json](registry/sources/facades/work/rois.json)
  - [registry/sources/facades/work/meas.json](registry/sources/facades/work/meas.json)
- Outputs:
  - [registry/sources/facades/work/aspect.json](registry/sources/facades/work/aspect.json)
- Network: no; deterministic: yes; kind: derive; after: `facade-measure`

#### 48. `facade-build` (registry/sources/facades/tools/build.py)

- Command: `cd cwplans/registry/sources/facades/work && python3 ../tools/build.py`
- Method: Writes the rectified patches at metric aspect and the bays, floors and colours in metres.
- Rules:
  - Floor height = height / floors (assumed) unless a face width gives a scale; bay = OSM face width / bay count when the whole face is visible.
- Inputs:
  - [registry/sources/facades/work/rois.json](registry/sources/facades/work/rois.json)
  - [registry/sources/facades/work/aspect.json](registry/sources/facades/work/aspect.json)
  - [registry/sources/facades/work/meas.json](registry/sources/facades/work/meas.json)
  - `data/raw/facades/<cwb-id>/src-<id>.jpg` (local, not committed)
- Outputs:
  - [registry/sources/facades/work/results.json](registry/sources/facades/work/results.json)
  - `data/raw/facades/<cwb-id>/rect-<key>.jpg` (local, not committed)
- Network: no; deterministic: yes; kind: derive; after: `facade-metric`
- Hand judgement: The floor count used to scale a patch is an assumed or judged value where no face width is visible.

#### 49. `facade-write` (registry/sources/facades/tools/write_facades.py)

- Command: `cd cwplans/registry/sources/facades/work && python3 ../tools/write_facades.py`
- Method: Joins the measurements, the footprint widths and the judged facts into one record per tower, each value marked measured, judged or assumed.
- Rules:
  - Judged facts and their sources are written in the script.
- Inputs:
  - [registry/sources/facades/work/results.json](registry/sources/facades/work/results.json)
  - [registry/sources/facades/work/footprints.json](registry/sources/facades/work/footprints.json)
  - [registry/sources/facades/photos.json](registry/sources/facades/photos.json)
- Outputs:
  - [registry/sources/facades/facades.json](registry/sources/facades/facades.json)
- Network: no; deterministic: yes; kind: derive; after: `facade-build`
- Hand judgement: Judged facts (by looking at photos) and their sources are written in write_facades.py; each value is marked measured, judged or assumed in facades.json.

#### 50. `build-facade-atlas` (tools/build-facade-atlas.py)

- Command: `python3 cwplans/tools/build-facade-atlas.py`
- Method: Cuts one tile per tower from its rectified open photo, holding whole floors and whole bays so it repeats along the walls, and packs the tiles into an 8 x 4 atlas of 256 px tiles.
- Rules:
  - One patch per tower, chosen by eye from data/raw/facades/contact.jpg; Citigroup Centre left out (crane, blur).
  - Tile = whole floors (about 40% of the patch, at most 25 m) by whole bays; size in metres from the measured floor height or bay width.
  - Attribution per tile from photos.json.
- Inputs:
  - [registry/sources/facades/work/results.json](registry/sources/facades/work/results.json)
  - [registry/sources/facades/facades.json](registry/sources/facades/facades.json)
  - [registry/sources/facades/photos.json](registry/sources/facades/photos.json)
  - `data/raw/facades/<cwb-id>/rect-<key>.jpg` (local, not committed)
- Outputs:
  - [docklands/data/tex/facades-registry.jpg](docklands/data/tex/facades-registry.jpg)
  - [docklands/data/tex/facades-registry.json](docklands/data/tex/facades-registry.json)
- Network: no; deterministic: yes; kind: derive; after: `facade-build`, `facade-write`
- Hand judgement: One patch per tower chosen by eye from data/raw/facades/contact.jpg; Citigroup Centre left out (crane, blur).

#### Hand step `facade-photos`: Open photos, facade regions and footprint widths for the facade measures

- By: facade agent, then the main session; date: 2026-10-03
- Method: Searched Wikimedia Commons and Flickr for photos of the Canary Wharf towers, downloaded only CC0, Public Domain Mark and CC BY images, recorded author, licence, page and the face shown; chose a facade region in each photo by eye; computed oriented bounding boxes of the OSM footprints for face widths.
- Rules:
  - CC BY-SA, NC, ND and all-rights-reserved images are not downloaded; seven CC BY-SA Commons photos were looked at for facts only.
  - The face shown is judged from landmarks when a photo has no geotag (32 of 45 Flickr photos).
  - Commons and Flickr rate-limit the container (429, retry after 600 s): wait, do not hammer.
- Inputs:
  - **osm** (OpenStreetMap, ODbL 1.0)
- Outputs:
  - [registry/sources/facades/photos.json](registry/sources/facades/photos.json)
  - [registry/sources/facades/work/rois.json](registry/sources/facades/work/rois.json)
  - [registry/sources/facades/work/footprints.json](registry/sources/facades/work/footprints.json)
- Decisions recorded in: registry/sources/facades/photos.json (face and how it was judged); registry/sources/facades/work/rois.json

### 18. Splats and drone

#### 51. `build-splats` (tools/build-splats.mjs)

- Command: `node cwplans/tools/build-splats.mjs [--ground 4] [--cell 7.2] [--box x0,x1,z0,z1]`
- Method: Synthesises Gaussian splats straight from the model (no training): ground discs coloured from the 2008 aerial photograph, roof discs, and wall discs with a glass band, written as .splat and as a 3DGS PLY.
- Rules:
  - Box: the model's focus box plus 300 m unless --box is given.
  - Ground discs every 4 m (--ground) where no building covers the cell, coloured bilinearly from rgb2008.ppm.
  - Roof discs every 4 m inside the outline: aerial colour on buildings under 40 m, else the building colour.
  - Walls: one disc per 3.6 m storey and 7.2 m (--cell) of wall, and one glass-band disc 3 cm in front per cell (buildings 4 m or taller).
  - The set entry 'cw-synth' replaces any earlier one in index.json; other entries are kept.
  - every splat records its building (uint16 index into cw-synth.json groups; 65535 = ground) for animation
  - Positions in model metres (x east, y up, z south); 32-byte .splat records, gzipped.
- Inputs:
  - [docklands/data/area.js](docklands/data/area.js)
  - [docklands/data/tex/textures.json](docklands/data/tex/textures.json)
  - `data/raw/imagery/rgb2008.ppm` (local, not committed)
  - [docklands/data/splats/index.json](docklands/data/splats/index.json)
- Outputs:
  - [docklands/data/splats/cw-synth.splat.gz](docklands/data/splats/cw-synth.splat.gz)
  - [docklands/data/splats/cw-synth.json](docklands/data/splats/cw-synth.json)
  - [docklands/data/splats/index.json](docklands/data/splats/index.json)
  - `data/raw/splats/cw-synth.ply` (local, not committed)
  - [docklands/data/splats/cw-synth.groups.bin.gz](docklands/data/splats/cw-synth.groups.bin.gz)
- Network: no; deterministic: no; kind: derive; after: `build-docklands`, `build-aerial-rgb2008`

#### 114. `drone-flight` (tools/drone-flight.mjs)

- Command: `node cwplans/tools/drone-flight.mjs [--out path.json] [--svg plan.svg] [--coverage 480]`
- Method: Plans a synthetic drone flight over the Canary Wharf estate in the 3D model for splat training captures, checks it against limits, and optionally writes the path, a plan and a coverage estimate.
- Rules:
  - Centripetal Catmull-Rom splines through eye and target waypoints.
  - Limits: 30 m from every building prism, 15 m above the terrain, altitude 40-350 m, pitch 10-70 degrees, yaw rate 15 degrees/s, along-track acceleration 0.6 m/s2, lateral 1.5 m/s2, turn rate 10 degrees/s; waypoints are raised until the clearances hold.
- Inputs:
  - [docklands/data/area.js](docklands/data/area.js)
  - [atlas/data/atlas.json](atlas/data/atlas.json)
- Outputs:
  - `<--out path.json>` (local, not committed)
  - `<--svg plan.svg>` (local, not committed)
- Network: no; deterministic: yes; kind: derive; after: `build-docklands`, `build-atlas`
- Hand judgement: The route (overview loop, spiral, dock passes, tower orbits, crossings) is designed by hand in plan(); only the clearance repair is automatic.
- Note: Also imported as a module by drone-capture.mjs.

#### 115. `drone-capture` (tools/drone-capture.mjs)

- Command: `python3 -m http.server 8765 --bind 127.0.0.1 & node cwplans/tools/drone-capture.mjs --run trial --frames 480`
- Method: Renders the drone flight in the Docklands 3D page and writes a Gaussian-splat training set: images, nerfstudio transforms, a COLMAP text model, an initial point cloud sampled from the model surfaces, per-frame building visibility, a pose check, a preview video and a contact sheet.
- Rules:
  - A building counts as seen in a frame when at least 30 of its pixels are visible in the pick layer.
  - Resumable: frames already in images/ and frames.jsonl are skipped unless --redo; a run with different size, field of view, ground or spacing is refused.
  - The initial point cloud is randomly jittered over terrain, water and building surfaces (Math.random).
- Inputs:
  - [docklands/index.html](docklands/index.html)
  - [docklands/data/area.js](docklands/data/area.js)
  - [docklands/data/under.js](docklands/data/under.js)
  - [atlas/data/atlas.json](atlas/data/atlas.json)
  - [docklands/data/tex/textures.json](docklands/data/tex/textures.json)
  - [docklands/data/tex/rgb2008.jpg](docklands/data/tex/rgb2008.jpg)
  - `<--path path.json>` (local, not committed)
- Outputs:
  - `data/raw/drone/<run>/images/frame_NNNNN.jpg` (local, not committed)
  - `data/raw/drone/<run>/frames.jsonl` (local, not committed)
  - `data/raw/drone/<run>/run.json` (local, not committed)
  - `data/raw/drone/<run>/transforms.json` (local, not committed)
  - `data/raw/drone/<run>/sparse/0/cameras.txt` (local, not committed)
  - `data/raw/drone/<run>/sparse/0/images.txt` (local, not committed)
  - `data/raw/drone/<run>/sparse/0/points3D.txt` (local, not committed)
  - `data/raw/drone/<run>/sparse_pc.ply` (local, not committed)
  - `data/raw/drone/<run>/coverage.json` (local, not committed)
  - `data/raw/drone/<run>/posecheck.json` (local, not committed)
  - `data/raw/drone/<run>/posecheck_NNNNN.jpg` (local, not committed)
  - `data/raw/drone/<run>/preview.mp4` (local, not committed)
  - `data/raw/drone/<run>/contact.jpg` (local, not committed)
- Network: no; deterministic: no; kind: render; after: `drone-flight`, `build-docklands`, `build-atlas`, `build-aerial-rgb2008`
- Note: Child processes: Chromium through Playwright, ffmpeg.

#### 116. `train-splat` (tools/train-splat.sh)

- Command: `cwplans/tools/train-splat.sh DATASET [-n 7000] [-d 2] [--sh 3] [--name NAME]`
- Method: Trains a 3D Gaussian splat on the CPU with OpenSplat from a nerfstudio dataset, then converts the result to .splat (ply-to-splat.mjs) and .sog (splat-transform).
- Rules:
  - Poses are passed to OpenSplat unchanged (OpenGL / nerfstudio camera-to-world; checked with splat-pose-test.py).
  - Defaults: 7000 iterations, image downscale 2, SH degree 3, at most 1,500,000 gaussians, refine every 500 steps.
- Inputs:
  - `DATASET/transforms.json` (local, not committed)
  - `DATASET/images/` (local, not committed)
  - `DATASET/sparse_pc.ply` (local, not committed)
  - `/opt/opensplat/build/opensplat` (local, not committed)
- Outputs:
  - `DATASET/NAME.ply` (local, not committed)
  - `DATASET/NAME.splat` (local, not committed)
  - `DATASET/NAME.sog` (local, not committed)
  - `DATASET/NAME.log` (local, not committed)
  - `DATASET/NAME.progress` (local, not committed)
- Network: no; deterministic: no; kind: derive; after: `drone-capture`
- Note: Child processes: opensplat, node ply-to-splat.mjs, splat-transform (falls back to 'npx -y @playcanvas/splat-transform', which downloads from npm when it is not installed). The log carries the run date.

#### 117. `ply-to-splat` (tools/ply-to-splat.mjs)

- Command: `node cwplans/tools/ply-to-splat.mjs in.ply out.splat`
- Method: Converts a 3DGS PLY to the antimatter15 32-byte .splat format.
- Rules:
  - scale = exp(log scale); rgb = 0.5 + SH_C0 x f_dc; alpha = sigmoid(opacity); quaternion normalised and stored as q x 128 + 128.
  - Sorted by exp(sum of log scales) x opacity, largest first; non-finite gaussians are dropped; f_rest is ignored.
- Inputs:
  - `<in.ply>` (local, not committed)
- Outputs:
  - `<out.splat>` (local, not committed)
- Network: no; deterministic: yes; kind: export; after: `train-splat`
- Note: Called by train-splat.sh; also runs alone.

#### 118. `publish-trained-splat` (tools/publish-trained-splat.mjs)

- Command: `node cwplans/tools/publish-trained-splat.mjs data/raw/drone/<run>/<name>.splat <set-name> "<title>"`
- Method: Puts a trained splat set on the 3D page: crops it to the area the drone frames cover, drops floaters and oversized splats, and gives each splat its model building for the music visualiser.
- Rules:
  - Keep splats within the box of the frame positions plus 150 m, between -30 and 260 m OD, with no axis over 25 m.
  - A splat belongs to a building when it lies inside the footprint (2 m grace) between the base - 2 m and the top + 6 m; else 65535.
  - Positions are not changed: train-splat.sh keeps the model frame (no --center).
- Inputs:
  - `data/raw/drone/<run>/<name>.splat` (local, not committed)
  - `data/raw/drone/<run>/transforms.json` (local, not committed)
  - [docklands/data/splats/cw-synth.json](docklands/data/splats/cw-synth.json)
  - [docklands/data/area.js](docklands/data/area.js)
- Outputs:
  - [docklands/data/splats/cw-trained.splat.gz](docklands/data/splats/cw-trained.splat.gz)
  - [docklands/data/splats/cw-trained.groups.bin.gz](docklands/data/splats/cw-trained.groups.bin.gz)
  - [docklands/data/splats/cw-trained.json](docklands/data/splats/cw-trained.json)
  - [docklands/data/splats/index.json](docklands/data/splats/index.json)
- Network: no; deterministic: yes; kind: derive; after: `train-splat`, `ply-to-splat`, `build-splats`
- Note: Run for the set cw-trained with data/raw/drone/cw-photo-480/first-n3000-d2.splat; another run adds its own set files.

### 19. Skyline

#### 44. `build-skyline` (tools/build-skyline.mjs)

- Command: `node cwplans/tools/build-skyline.mjs`
- Method: Measures the height of each model building on the Canary Wharf estate and 300 m round it in every EA LiDAR surface model from 1999 to 2022, for the year slider.
- Rules:
  - height = 90th percentile of the DSM inside today's OSM outline (1 m sampling, at most 4000 points) minus the model ground
  - not standing: under 3 m or under a quarter of today's height (a cleared site with hoardings)
  - null: under 30% of the outline's points have data in that survey
  - a survey that flew under 20% of the buildings is left out (2015)
  - Heights in metres; the ground is the model ground (area.js terrain), not each survey's DTM.
- Inputs:
  - `data/raw/dsm/<product>-<year>-<res>-<tile>.zip` (local, not committed)
  - **ea-dsm-history** (Environment Agency LiDAR DSM survey tiles 1999 to 2022, OGL v3.0): https://environment.data.gov.uk/tiles/collections/survey/<product>/<year>/<res>/<tile>; DSM tiles TQ3575 and TQ3580 for 1999, 2003, 2007, 2012, 2015, 2018, 2020, 2022 (fetched by tools/fetch-dsm.sh, not by this tool) [manual download]
  - [docklands/data/area.js](docklands/data/area.js)
- Outputs:
  - [docklands/data/skyline.json](docklands/data/skyline.json)
- Network: no; deterministic: no; kind: derive; after: `build-docklands`, `fetch-dsm`
- Note: Child processes: unzip, find.

### 20. Sky, time, weather and tide

#### 53. `fit-tide-harmonics` (tools/fit-tide-harmonics.mjs)

- Command: `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=1 node cwplans/tools/fit-tide-harmonics.mjs [--archive-days 371] [--archive-step 7] [--archive-weekday 2|all] [--no-fetch]`
- Method: Fits a mean and tidal harmonic constituents by least squares to the Environment Agency 15-minute tidal levels of the three gauges round the 3D model, so that the 3D pages can show the tide at any time (the EA publishes readings, not predictions; PLA and UKHO predictions are not open). Two stages: a fetch stage (network, cached) and a fit stage that reads only the cached files.
- Rules:
  - Readings: the API readings (about four weeks) and one archive day in seven back to a year (no archive file for some days: skipped); since 2026-10-10 the fit reads only archive days of one weekday (--archive-weekday, default 2 = Tuesday, the days of the committed fit), because build-tide-history caches every day in the same folder and the held-out days of docklands/test/history-check.mjs (Fridays) must stay out of the fit; one request at a time; three retries 3, 6 and 9 s apart on timeouts and 5xx; User-Agent glitchcan-cwplans/0.1; cached files are not fetched again.
  - Cleaning: a reading is dropped when its difference from the nearest other gauge departs more than 0.45 m from the median of that difference over 2 h either side (docklands/sky.js cleanGauges, fault F21); after a first fit, readings with a residual over 1.2 m are dropped (gauge faults and the largest surges).
  - Constituents, in priority order M2, S2, N2, K1, O1, M4, MS4, MN4, M6, K2, P1, L2, NU2, MU2, 2N2, Q1, MK4, S4, 2MS6, 2MN6, M3, MK3, MO3, J1, OO1, M8, 3MS8, 2SM6, T2, MSf, Mf, Mm, Ssa, Sa; one is kept only when its speed differs from every kept one by at least 360 degrees over the span of the readings (Rayleigh criterion 1).
  - Arguments: mean longitudes of the Moon, Sun, lunar perigee, node and solar perigee (Meeus), tau = 15 deg/h x UT + h - s; nodal factors f and u by the formulas of Pugh (1987, table 4.3); compound tides from their parts; L2 takes the M2 factors (a simplification). Phases are lags on these arguments, not Admiralty phases. The code is docklands/tide.js, which the 3D page uses too.
  - Validation: a fit without 3 and 4 October 2026 (UTC) and the last 48 h of readings is compared with them (rms, maximum, high and low water height and time); the constituents written use all readings. Units m AOD, phases in degrees, amplitudes to 0.1 mm.
  - Output also holds the cleaned readings of the last 7 days (15-minute slots, null where none), so the page shows a measured level where it has one, and check values (predictions every 6 h on 10 October 2026) that the page compares with its own prediction.
- Inputs:
  - **ea-flood-monitoring** (Environment Agency real-time flood-monitoring API: tidal level readings at Tower Pier, OGL v3.0): https://environment.data.gov.uk/flood-monitoring/id/measures/{station}-level-tidal_level-i-15_min-mAOD/readings; stations 0007 Tower Pier, 0003 Charlton, 0001 Silvertown; startdate=today-45 days, enddate=today, _sorted, _limit=10000 (the API keeps about four weeks); plus /id/stations/{station} for the position
  - **ea-flood-monitoring** (Environment Agency real-time flood-monitoring API: tidal level readings at Tower Pier, OGL v3.0): https://environment.data.gov.uk/flood-monitoring/archive/readings-{YYYY-MM-DD}.csv; one daily archive file (all stations, about 60 MB) every 7 days from 10 to 371 days back, streamed; only the lines of the three tidal-level measures are kept
  - `data/raw/river/tide/api-{date}.json, data/raw/river/tide/archive-{date}.csv (the cached readings; their SHA-256 is written into the output)` (local, not committed)
- Outputs:
  - [docklands/data/sky/tide-harmonics.json](docklands/data/sky/tide-harmonics.json)
- Network: yes; deterministic: no; kind: derive; after: nothing
- Hand judgement: The choice of constituents, the cleaning thresholds and the one-day-in-seven archive sampling are stated defaults (this activity's rules); the foreshore profiles drawn from the prediction are defaults in docklands/layers/tide.js.
- Faults: [F21](https://github.com/danbri/londat/blob/main/cwplans/skills/docklands-data-curation/SKILL.md#fault-register)

#### 54. `build-tide-history` (tools/build-tide-history.mjs)

- Command: `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=1 node cwplans/tools/build-tide-history.mjs [--days 371] [--end YYYY-MM-DD] [--no-fetch]`
- Method: Collects the measured Environment Agency 15-minute tidal levels of the three gauges round the 3D model for every day of the last year, so that the Three.js port shows measured levels, not the harmonic prediction, for clock times older than the flood-monitoring API keeps. Two stages: a fetch stage (network, cached) and a build stage that reads only the cached files.
- Rules:
  - One request at a time; the whole stream is retried on a failure (4 tries, 2, 4, 8 s apart), and each GET is retried on timeouts and 5xx (3, 6, 9 s); User-Agent glitchcan-cwplans/0.1; a day is cached only when its stream is complete; cached days are not fetched again; a day with no archive file (HTTP 404) is cached as empty and listed in days.archive_missing.
  - Encoding: per gauge one array on a 15-minute grid from the first day 00:00 UTC; the value is round(m AOD x 100); null where the archive has no reading; readings off the 15-minute grid are counted and dropped.
  - No cleaning: the page drops a reading more than 1.5 m from its prediction (docklands/tide.js mergeReadings, SURGE.BAD), as for every other source of readings.
- Inputs:
  - **ea-flood-monitoring** (Environment Agency real-time flood-monitoring API: tidal level readings at Tower Pier, OGL v3.0): https://environment.data.gov.uk/flood-monitoring/archive/readings-{YYYY-MM-DD}.csv; every daily archive file (all stations, about 60 MB) from --end (default yesterday, UTC) back --days days, newest first, streamed; only the lines of the three tidal-level measures (0007, 0003, 0001 -level-tidal_level-i-15_min-mAOD) are kept (code shared with fit-tide-harmonics: tools/ea-tide-archive.mjs)
  - `data/raw/river/tide/archive-{date}.csv (the cached lines, shared with fit-tide-harmonics; their SHA-256 is written into the output)` (local, not committed)
- Outputs:
  - [docklands/data/sky/tide-history.json](docklands/data/sky/tide-history.json)
- Network: yes; deterministic: no; kind: derive; after: nothing
- Hand judgement: None beyond the encoding (whole centimetres: the gauges report mm; the page interpolates between 15-minute readings, rms 0.03 to 0.04 m over 30 minutes).
- Faults: 

#### 55. `fetch-sky` (tools/fetch-sky.mjs)

- Command: `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=1 node cwplans/tools/fetch-sky.mjs [stars] [lines] [sats] [weather] [tide] [names] [messier] [clouds [2026-10-03T23:00Z]] [lcy] [--date 2026-10-03]`
- Method: Fetches the star catalogue, the constellation lines, satellite orbital elements, hourly weather and tide-gauge readings for the Sky panel of the 3D page (docklands/sky.js) and writes small JSON snapshots; the weather and tide files cover the London date given and the next day (the owner's photo evening, 3 October 2026).
- Rules:
  - Stars: Bright Star Catalogue rows with a J2000 position and V <= 5.5 (2,887); columns from the CDS ReadMe: HR 1-4, RA 76-83, Dec 84-90, Vmag 103-107, B-V 110-114; RA and Dec to 0.001 degree, V and B-V to 0.01; a missing B-V becomes 0.6; sorted by V.
  - Constellation lines: every line string kept; longitude turned into RA 0-360 degrees; coordinates rounded to 0.01 degree. The Milky Way outlines of the same repository are not fetched (no stated licence); the page computes the band from galactic coordinates.
  - Satellites: the three requests merged, one record per NORAD catalogue number; OMM fields kept as published. CelesTrak keeps no history, so a snapshot holds the elements current at fetch time (run within a day or two of the date). One request per group per run (CelesTrak: at most one download per 2 hours).
  - Weather: the forecast API's past hours (best_match model, UK Met Office UKV first for London; it has visibility, which the ERA5 archive lacks); values as published, hourly, London time.
  - Tide: 15-minute instantaneous readings in m AOD, UTC times, sorted; no gap filling; EA gives readings only, no predictions. The page (sky.js cleanGauges) leaves out a reading whose difference from the nearest other gauge departs more than 0.45 m from the median of that difference over 2 h either side (fault F21), then interpolates linearly by chainage along data/river.json between the gauges. The EA has no tidal gauge between Tower Pier and Charlton (station search of 2026-10-04, 15 km round the estate).
  - Star names: rows of IAU-CSN whose designation is an HR number present in stars.json (324 of 451 names); columns found by the positions of the header titles (Name/Diacritics, Designation, Con), because names can hold spaces; the diacritic form is kept.
  - Clouds: the EUMETSAT cloud mask PNG kept as sent. The page (sky.js) reads white pixels (R, G, B > 200) as cloud, takes the mean over 60 km round the box centre, and raises each layer's Open-Meteo cover by 0.9 x (local mask - mean), clamped to 0..1, so the mask places the cloud and Open-Meteo keeps the amount; the mask moves with 2.2 x the 10 m wind for up to 90 minutes from its slot. Other times only with Fetch ticked (EUMETView sends CORS headers; the mask appears about 25 minutes after the slot; none before 2020-09-01). Parallax (about 1.6 km north for cloud at 1 km over London) is not corrected.
  - London City Airport: the landing thresholds are the two ends of the main runway way (ref 09/27; its displaced-threshold ways are left out); the page draws each approach as the extended centreline rising at 5.5 degrees (the published glide path) from 15 m over the threshold (50 ft assumed) out to 12 km. No live aircraft: OpenSky (written licence for any live product), adsb.fi (personal non-commercial), ADS-B Exchange (commercial), adsb.lol (ODbL share-alike) and airplanes.live (terms behind a bot challenge) were checked on 2026-10-04.
  - Messier: all 109 rows of the HEASARC table (M 102 is not in it); RA and Dec from sexagesimal J2000 to degrees (0.001); size = the larger dimension in arcmin; the notes column title-cased as the common name. OpenNGC was not used (CC BY-SA).
  - Network: User-Agent glitchcan-cwplans/0.1; three retries 3, 6 and 9 s apart on timeouts and 5xx (the agent proxy drops connections now and then); 4xx is not retried; files overwritten on every run.
- Inputs:
  - **yale-bsc5** (Yale Bright Star Catalogue, 5th revised edition, public domain (NASA Astronomical Data Center); CDS asks that catalogues be cited): https://cdsarc.cds.unistra.fr/ftp/V/50/catalog.gz; the whole catalogue, fixed-width text (gzip)
  - **d3-celestial** (d3-celestial data/constellations.lines.json, BSD-3-Clause (repository licence, Copyright (c) 2015 Olaf Frohn); lines after the IAU constellation charts): https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ofrohn/d3-celestial/master/data/constellations.lines.json; GeoJSON MultiLineString per constellation
  - **celestrak** (CelesTrak GP element sets, no licence stated; US Government (18 SDS / Space-Track) orbital data republished by CelesTrak; usage policy: download only when needed, at most once per 2 hours, check errors): https://celestrak.org/NORAD/elements/gp.php; CATNR=25544 (ISS), CATNR=48274 (Tiangong), GROUP=visual; FORMAT=json (OMM)
  - **open-meteo** (Open-Meteo weather API, CC BY 4.0 (data); API free for non-commercial use): https://api.open-meteo.com/v1/forecast; latitude=51.505, longitude=-0.02, start_date=DATE, end_date=DATE+1, hourly cloud cover (total, low, mid, high), visibility, humidity, dew point, precipitation, weather code, temperature, wind; timezone Europe/London; model best_match
  - **ea-flood-monitoring** (Environment Agency real-time flood-monitoring API: tidal level readings at Tower Pier, OGL v3.0): https://environment.data.gov.uk/flood-monitoring/id/measures/{station}-level-tidal_level-i-15_min-mAOD/readings; stations 0007 Tower Pier, 0003 Charlton, 0001 Silvertown; startdate=DATE, enddate=DATE+2, _sorted, _limit=1000; plus /id/stations/{station} for the position
  - **iau-wgsn** (IAU Catalog of Star Names, CC BY (the file: all IAU-produced products are released under Creative Commons Attribution, free to use as long as the source is mentioned); names are facts): https://www.pas.rochester.edu/~emamajek/WGSN/IAU-CSN.txt; the whole IAU Catalog of Star Names, fixed-column text (UTF-8)
  - **heasarc-messier** (HEASARC MESSIER table, NASA HEASARC service, US Government work: no copyright claimed (public domain); positions, sizes and magnitudes are facts): https://heasarc.gsfc.nasa.gov/db-perl/W3Browse/w3query.pl; tablehead=name=heasarc_messier, Action=Query, ResultMax=0, displaymode=BatchDisplay, Fields=All, Coordinates=Equatorial, Equinox=2000 (pipe-separated text)
  - **eumetsat-clm** (EUMETSAT Meteosat, CC BY 4.0: a Meteosat Derived Product is Core data (EUMETSAT Data Policy, last amended 2024-2026, Articles 4 to 6: free and unrestricted, redistribution allowed)): https://view.eumetsat.int/geoserver/wms; WMS 1.3.0 GetMap, layers=msg_fes:clm, crs=EPSG:4326, bbox=50.5,-1.6,52.5,1.6, 256 x 256, image/png, time=the 15-minute slot (default DATE 23:00Z)
  - **osm** (OpenStreetMap, ODbL 1.0): data/raw/docklands/greater_london-latest.osm.pbf (local, extract osmfr-greater-london); osmium extract -b 0.03,51.495,0.08,51.51, tags-filter w/aeroway=runway, export geojson; the way with ref 09/27
- Outputs:
  - [docklands/data/sky/stars.json](docklands/data/sky/stars.json)
  - [docklands/data/sky/constellation-lines.json](docklands/data/sky/constellation-lines.json)
  - [docklands/data/sky/sats-2026-10-03.json](docklands/data/sky/sats-2026-10-03.json)
  - [docklands/data/sky/weather-2026-10-03.json](docklands/data/sky/weather-2026-10-03.json)
  - [docklands/data/sky/tide-2026-10-03.json](docklands/data/sky/tide-2026-10-03.json)
  - [docklands/data/sky/star-names.json](docklands/data/sky/star-names.json)
  - [docklands/data/sky/messier.json](docklands/data/sky/messier.json)
  - [docklands/data/sky/clm-20261003T2300Z.png](docklands/data/sky/clm-20261003T2300Z.png)
  - [docklands/data/sky/lcy-approach.json](docklands/data/sky/lcy-approach.json)
- Network: yes; deterministic: no; kind: fetch; after: nothing
- Hand judgement: Source choice and licence decisions (BSC5 as public domain, d3-celestial lines as BSD-3, CelesTrak with no stated licence and flagged for review, Vieira Milky Way outlines refused) are recorded in data-register.json sources and docklands/README.md "Sky, time, weather and tide".

#### 86. `fetch-adsb-cache` (tools/fetch-adsb-cache.mjs)

- Command: `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=1 node cwplans/tools/fetch-adsb-cache.mjs --out <dir> [--restore] [--push] [--days 7] [--date YYYY-MM-DD]`
- Method: Streams the tar parts of each missing UTC day of the last 7 (a minimal tar reader; no file is written), gunzips each trace_full_<hex>.json, skips a trace with no latitude 51.x, parses it, and keeps the points within 25 nm of 51.505 N 0.02 W. Splits them into legs (the readsb new-leg flag, a gap of more than 10 minutes in the area, a change of callsign). Height: GNSS (alt_geom, ft above the WGS84 ellipsoid) when sent, else pressure altitude plus the hour's median (GNSS - pressure) of the area's aircraft below 10,000 ft; on the ground: flag. Writes one gzip JSON per UTC hour with the legs that overlap it (2 minutes before for trails, one point after), delta-coded integer columns [t s, lat and lon 1e-5 deg, alt 25 ft, height source, ground speed kt, track deg], a day.json with counts, index.json and a README with the ODbL notice; removes days older than 7. --push builds the orphan commit of the folder with a temporary index (GIT_INDEX_FILE, git write-tree, git commit-tree) and force-pushes it to refs/heads/adsb-cache, so the branch always has one commit.
- Rules:
  - Privacy rule of docklands/planes-live.js identified(): a callsign is kept only for an operator ICAO callsign (three letters and a digit) without the LADD or PIA flag (dbFlags 8, 4) and with an ICAO address (no '~'); every other leg gets an anonymous key (x<n>) and keeps only its type and category. No registration, owner (ownOp), squawk or ICAO address of an unidentified aircraft is written.
  - Thinning: at most one point a second; above 15,000 ft at most one a minute (the 3D page shows high traffic as context only).
  - Not on main: the cache is on the orphan branch adsb-cache with one commit, so git history does not grow each day; the main working tree and index are never touched.
  - Licence: ODbL 1.0 (owner approval 2026-10-09 for the committed cache); the branch README and every page view carry 'Aircraft: © adsb.lol contributors, ODbL'.
  - Politeness: one download at a time, User-Agent naming this repository; a day whose release is not yet published is skipped and fetched by the next run.
- Inputs:
  - **adsb-lol** (adsb.lol aircraft: the live API, ODbL 1.0 (share-alike): OpenAPI info.license 'Open Data Commons Open Database License (ODbL) v1.0'; 'The license for the API as well as all data ADSB.lol makes public is ODbL'; each history release README: 'This database is made available under the Open Database License' (feeders waive their rights under CC0). API free to use, no key yet ('In the future, you will require an API key'); rate limits 'dynamic', not published): https://github.com/adsblol/globe_history_<year>/releases/download/v<YYYY.MM.DD>-planes-readsb-prod-0/v<YYYY.MM.DD>-planes-readsb-prod-0.tar.aa (.ab, .ac, ...); one streamed download of each 2 GB part per new day (about 4.2 GB a day, nothing kept on disk); a HEAD request first to see whether the day's release exists
  - `branch adsb-cache of danbri/londat (with --restore: the days already held, by git archive)` (local, not committed)
- Outputs:
  - `branch adsb-cache: index.json, README.md, adsb/<YYYY-MM-DD>/day.json and adsb/<YYYY-MM-DD>/<HH>.json.gz (data-register.json 'branches')` (local, not committed)
- Network: yes; deterministic: yes; kind: fetch; after: nothing
- Note: skill docklands-sky, 'Live aircraft in the Three.js port'; workflow .github/workflows/adsb-cache.yml (daily)

#### 87. `fetch-live-cache` (tools/fetch-live-cache.mjs)

- Command: `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=1 node cwplans/tools/fetch-live-cache.mjs --out <dir> [--restore] [--push] [--only aircraft|ships|metar]`
- Method: Asks the three sources once each and adds the answers to rolling buffers. Aircraft: each report [t = answer time - seen_pos, lat, lon, altitude above the WGS84 ellipsoid (alt_geom, else alt_baro + the answer's median (alt_geom - alt_baro) below 10,000 ft), height source, ground speed, track, vertical rate, turn rate], the last 60 minutes. Ships: the newest position of each kept vessel added when it moved or 30 minutes passed; positions of the last 2 hours, the newest kept at any age while Open Waters lists the vessel. METAR: the reports of the last 24 hours with visibility in metres from the report text. A source that fails keeps its previous file. --push builds the orphan commit with a temporary index (GIT_INDEX_FILE, git write-tree, git commit-tree) and force-pushes refs/heads/live-cache, so the branch always has one commit.
- Rules:
  - Aircraft privacy rule of docklands/planes-live.js identified(): callsign and ICAO address (the key) only for an operator ICAO callsign without LADD/PIA and with an ICAO address; every other aircraft gets an anonymous key x<n>, joined to its earlier anonymous reports only by type and a dead-reckoned position (tolerance 1.5 km + 30 % of the distance flown, nearest first, within 20 minutes), never by address; no registration, owner or squawk.
  - Ships: the licence filter of fetch-ais.mjs (CC0, NLOD, CC BY kept; AISHub and aisstream.io kept for scoping, owner 2026-10-04; others dropped and counted) and its small-craft rule (ITU 36/37, class B without a commercial type, class and type not heard: counted in snaps[].private_not_listed, never listed).
  - Ground vehicles and obstacles (ADS-B category C, type TWR/GND/SERV) and reports with seen_pos over 60 s are not kept.
  - Not on main: one commit on the orphan branch live-cache, so git history does not grow; the main working tree and index are never touched.
  - Licences: ODbL 1.0 (adsb.lol), per event (Open Waters AIS), public domain (NOAA AWC METAR); the branch README carries the notices.
- Inputs:
  - **adsb-lol** (adsb.lol aircraft: the live API, ODbL 1.0 (share-alike): OpenAPI info.license 'Open Data Commons Open Database License (ODbL) v1.0'; 'The license for the API as well as all data ADSB.lol makes public is ODbL'; each history release README: 'This database is made available under the Open Database License' (feeders waive their rights under CC0). API free to use, no key yet ('In the future, you will require an API key'); rate limits 'dynamic', not published): https://api.adsb.lol/v2/point/51.505/-0.02/25; one GET a run (every 5 minutes), User-Agent naming this repository; 3 tries on 429, 5xx and timeouts
  - **openwaters-ais** (Open Waters AIS, Per event, the licence of the upstream source (the aggregate is not relicensed). Only events from open sources are kept (see openwaters-volunteer-cc0); AISHub and aisstream.io events are not kept (owner decision). Service: free tiers 'for personal use'; commercial use of the hosted service is a paid tier): https://ais.openwaters.io/v1/vessels?bbox=51.474,-0.095,51.528,0.085; one GET a run, anonymous tier
  - **awc-metar** (London City Airport, NOAA/NWS: 'The information on National Weather Service (NWS) Web pages are in the public domain, unless specifically noted otherwise' (https://www.weather.gov/disclaimer, read 2026-10-09). The reports are made by the UK Met Office for the aerodrome and exchanged through the WMO (essential data, WMO Resolution 40). API terms (https://aviationweather.gov/data/api/): 'keep requests limited in scope and frequency', at most 100 requests a minute; one request each 5 minutes is used): https://aviationweather.gov/api/data/metar?ids=EGLC&format=json&hours=24; one GET a run
  - `branch live-cache of danbri/londat (with --restore: the previous aircraft.json, ships.json and metar.json, by git fetch --depth=1 and git show)` (local, not committed)
- Outputs:
  - `branch live-cache: README.md, aircraft.json, ships.json, metar.json (data-register.json 'branches')` (local, not committed)
- Network: yes; deterministic: yes; kind: fetch; after: nothing
- Note: skills docklands-sky ('Live aircraft in the Three.js port', 'METAR (London City Airport)') and cwplans-river-and-water ('AIS: Open Waters'); workflow .github/workflows/live-cache.yml (every 5 minutes)

#### 126. `solve-photo-sun` (tools/solve-photo-sun.mjs)

- Command: `node cwplans/tools/solve-photo-sun.mjs [docklands/reference/day-2026-10-04/points.json]`
- Method: Solves a daytime photo's camera and capture time: a pinhole camera fitted to tower tops of the model, shadows on a level deck back-projected through it, and the sun azimuth matched to a time with astronomy-engine. Prints JSON; the results are copied by hand into the photo folder's README and the docklands-sky skill.
- Rules:
  - Camera: heading (from grid north), tilt, roll and focal length by Nelder-Mead least squares on the landmark pixels; principal point fixed at the image centre; landmark = the apex (pyramid) or the centroid of the top tier at its top height in docklands/data/towers.json; a landmark with a null y is fitted in x only.
  - Shadow: the pixel of a post foot and of the shadow of its cap are back-projected to the plane 1.5 m below the eye; sun grid azimuth = bearing foot->shadow + 180 degrees; true azimuth = grid + grid_to_true_deg (1.54 here). The azimuth does not depend on the assumed camera height; the printed shadow lengths do (given for 1.3, 1.5, 1.7 m).
  - Time: astronomy-engine Equator(of date, aberration) + Horizon(refraction "normal") for the observer in the points file; bisection on the azimuth between 06:00 and 18:00 UTC of the date. Result = the time of the mean azimuth; range = the times of the smallest and largest azimuth.
  - The library is run in a vm context; times are passed as that realm's Date (its instanceof check fails on a Date of the main realm).
  - The 3D page view ?view=greenlandday uses the same four landmarks refitted with roll 0 (the page camera has no roll): heading 51.9, tilt 6.45, field 99.9, rms 2.7 px (fitted by a grid search in the session, numbers in the docklands-3d-page skill).
- Inputs:
  - [docklands/reference/day-2026-10-04/points.json](docklands/reference/day-2026-10-04/points.json)
  - [docklands/data/towers.json](docklands/data/towers.json)
  - [docklands/vendor/astronomy.browser.min.js](docklands/vendor/astronomy.browser.min.js)
- Outputs: none (prints only)
- Network: no; deterministic: yes; kind: derive; after: `build-towers`
- Hand judgement: The pixel positions in points.json are read by eye from 2x and 4x enlargements (which point is the foot of a post is a judgement: the base plate hides it); recorded in points.json and the README.

#### 127. `solve-photo-view` (../tools/view-mcp/solve.mjs)

- Command: `node tools/view-mcp/solve.mjs cwplans/docklands/reference/plane-2026-10/points.json [--k1] --out cwplans/docklands/reference/plane-2026-10/solution.json`
- Method: Solves the camera of any photo of the zone, ground or air: a pinhole camera with eye position, heading, pitch, roll, focal length and optional radial distortion k1, fitted by Levenberg-Marquardt (numeric Jacobian) to landmark pixels (tower tops, named places, given coordinates), shoreline pixels (distance to the projected OSM water outline) and horizon pixels (the sea-level dip from the eye height). Reports rms, formal 1-sigma per parameter from the covariance, per-point residuals and hold-out residuals. Library tools/view-mcp/view-lib.mjs, also served as the docklands-view MCP (tools/view-mcp/server.mjs).
- Rules:
  - Model metres: x = E - 537550, z = -(N - 180300), y = m OD; WGS84 by the page's own quadratic fit (meta.geo) and its Newton inverse.
  - Tower landmark = the apex (towers.json top.apex) or the centroid of the top tier at its top height; place landmark = area.js places at its ground height.
  - Earth curvature is applied to every point (drop d^2/2R with refraction k = 0.13); the horizon target is the sea-level dip -sqrt(2h/R') with weight 0.5 (the visible horizon is hills 20 to 40 km away).
  - Hold-out points are projected but not fitted; the run restarts once from its own result.
  - The page view takes the fitted camera without k1 (the page has no lens distortion): eyeView(eye, heading, pitch, hfov) plus roll.
- Inputs:
  - [docklands/reference/plane-2026-10/points.json](docklands/reference/plane-2026-10/points.json)
  - [docklands/data/towers.json](docklands/data/towers.json)
  - [docklands/data/area.js](docklands/data/area.js)
- Outputs:
  - [docklands/reference/plane-2026-10/solution.json](docklands/reference/plane-2026-10/solution.json)
  - [docklands/reference/plane-2026-10/compare-photo-render.jpg](docklands/reference/plane-2026-10/compare-photo-render.jpg)
- Network: no; deterministic: yes; kind: derive; after: `build-docklands`, `build-towers`
- Hand judgement: Pixel positions and which photo feature is which landmark are read by eye from grid enlargements; recorded in points.json and the photo folder's README (with the failed hold-out).

### 21. Crown lighting

#### 61. `fetch-crown-lighting` (tools/fetch-crown-lighting.mjs)

- Command: `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=1 node cwplans/tools/fetch-crown-lighting.mjs [--press] [--commons] [--thumbs] [--no-fetch]`
- Method: Finds the press pages and dated open photos that can say which colour a Canary Wharf crown (One Canada Square's halo first) showed on a given night: CWG sitemaps filtered by slug, archived copies of those pages, and Wikimedia Commons night photos with capture date and licence. The colour itself is judged by hand (activity crown-lighting-judgement).
- Rules:
  - Slug rule: lit, illuminated, halo, pyramid, turns-<colour>, lights-up-<colour>, light-it-, goes-<colour> (the regex is written into press-pages.json); plus a hand list of 5 pages found by search.
  - Pages are read only as Internet Archive id_ copies (cwg.com and canarywharf.com answer scripts with an Imperva challenge; their sitemaps are served and robots.txt allows them). Kept in the committed file: URL, slug date (ddmmyy code at the end of the slug), archive URL, title, published time, the colour words that occur in sentences naming One Canada Square, a pyramid, halo, crown or tower. Page text stays in the local cache.
  - Commons: a search hit is kept only if its title or description names the place (Canary Wharf, Canada Square, Docklands, Isle of Dogs, West India, Millwall, Blackwall, Cabot Square, Poplar); category members are kept. Use = fact only for CC BY-SA or GFDL; no image is committed.
  - Politeness: one request at a time; at least 3 s per host for cwg.com and web.archive.org, 2 s for archive.org and the Commons API, 3 s for upload.wikimedia.org; up to 4 tries with backoff (Retry-After, else 10, 30, 90 s, at most 600 s) on 429, 5xx and resets; 2 tries for an archive copy; thumbnails stop at the first refusal (2026-10-04: 429 after 31). Failures are written to data/raw/registry/lighting/log-<date>.txt and to the page status.
  - User-Agent glitchcan-cwplans/0.1 (https://github.com/danbri/londat).
- Inputs:
  - **cwg-press** (Canary Wharf Group press releases and community news, none stated (all rights reserved by default); titles, dates and facts only): https://cwg.com/press-release-sitemap.xml, https://cwg.com/community_news-sitemap.xml (direct); https://web.archive.org/web/2026id_/https://canarywharf.com/news-sitemap.xml; Yoast sitemaps; every <loc> outside /wp-content/
  - **cwg-press** (Canary Wharf Group press releases and community news, none stated (all rights reserved by default); titles, dates and facts only): https://archive.org/wayback/available?url=… then https://web.archive.org/web/{timestamp}id_/{url}; the closest archived copy of each candidate page, under group.canarywharf.com and cwg.com
  - **commons-meta** (Wikimedia Commons file metadata, metadata CC0 (structured data) / facts; each photo under its own licence (mostly CC BY-SA: colour judged by eye, image not copied)): https://commons.wikimedia.org/w/api.php; generator=categorymembers (Category:One Canada Square at night, Category:Canary Wharf at night) and generator=search in the File namespace (4 searches, filetype:bitmap); prop=imageinfo, extmetadata DateTimeOriginal|LicenseShortName|Artist|ImageDescription, iiurlwidth=960; continue followed
  - **commons-meta** (Wikimedia Commons file metadata, metadata CC0 (structured data) / facts; each photo under its own licence (mostly CC BY-SA: colour judged by eye, image not copied)): https://upload.wikimedia.org/ (thumburl); 960 px thumbnails, kept locally only
- Outputs:
  - [registry/sources/lighting/press-pages.json](registry/sources/lighting/press-pages.json)
  - [registry/sources/lighting/commons-photos.json](registry/sources/lighting/commons-photos.json)
  - `data/raw/registry/lighting/ (sitemaps, archived pages, colour sentences, Commons API answers, thumbnails, request log)` (local, not committed)
- Network: yes; deterministic: no; kind: fetch; after: nothing
- Hand judgement: Which pages to add to the hand list (KNOWN in the tool) after a web search; recorded in press-pages.json meta.known_pages.

#### Hand step `crown-lighting-judgement`: Crown lighting policy, campaigns and dated colour observations

- By: hand (crown lighting agent); date: 2026-10-04
- Method: Web searches for how the colour of One Canada Square's top is chosen, its lighting systems, campaigns and contractors; each fact written in our own words with its URL, date and how it was read. Commons thumbnails and the owner's photos of 3 October 2026 looked at by eye: what is lit (pyramid faces, halo band, facade wash, nothing) and its colour; an approximate hex from the mean of the brightest 20% of pixels in the lit area (30% for dim pyramids).
- Rules:
  - No text or image copied: facts only from all-rights-reserved pages and Wikipedia (CC BY-SA); CC BY-SA photos give URL, capture date and the colour seen.
  - A campaign needs a press, council, partner or designer source with a date; an observation needs a dated photo, post or report of the night itself.
  - A colour seen with no campaign source stays an observation with the reason unknown (3 October 2026); gaps between dates are never filled.
  - The apex aviation light (white, flashing) is never a campaign colour.
  - Confidence low when the pyramid is small, dim or in cloud; EXIF capture time is the photographer's local clock.
- Inputs:
  - [registry/sources/lighting/press-pages.json](registry/sources/lighting/press-pages.json)
  - [registry/sources/lighting/commons-photos.json](registry/sources/lighting/commons-photos.json)
  - [docklands/reference/night-2026-10-03/promenade-skyline-bollard.jpg](docklands/reference/night-2026-10-03/promenade-skyline-bollard.jpg)
  - [docklands/reference/night-2026-10-03/promenade-skyline-railing.jpg](docklands/reference/night-2026-10-03/promenade-skyline-railing.jpg)
  - **lighting-refs** (pages about the lighting of the Canary Wharf towers, all rights reserved, or CC BY-SA 4.0 for Wikipedia text: facts in our own words and URLs only, no text copied)
  - **social-posts** (public social media posts, platform terms; link and fact only, no text or image copied)
- Outputs:
  - [registry/sources/lighting/crown-lighting.json](registry/sources/lighting/crown-lighting.json)
  - [registry/sources/lighting/README.md](registry/sources/lighting/README.md)
- Decisions recorded in: registry/sources/lighting/crown-lighting.json (read, confidence, hex_kind per record); skills/cwplans-crown-lighting/SKILL.md
- Not committed as code: The pixel measurements were run from the session scratchpad (a 10-line Pillow script: crop, sort by brightness, mean of the top fraction); the boxes are in no committed file.

### 22. Feeds and events

#### 1. `fetch-works` (tools/fetch-works.mjs)

- Command: `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=1 node cwplans/tools/fetch-works.mjs [tfl-lines tfl-bus tfl-road street-manager gazette th-licences planning markets venue-events] [--no-fetch] [--refresh] [--days=90] [--month=YYYY-MM]`
- Method: Fetches public permits, works, closures, planned events and markets for the Docklands zone (the 3D model box plus the Royal Docks margin) from TfL, DfT Street Manager, The Gazette, Tower Hamlets, the Planning London Datahub and OSM, and harvests venue and council event programmes from the event feeds in feeds/events.json; writes one dated snapshot per source ({meta, items}) and works.json, every item in one normalised shape, for feeds/whatson.html.
- Rules:
  - Zone: "model" = WGS84 -0.095, 51.474 to 0.015, 51.522 (BOX_WGS84 of fetch-docklands.mjs; the tool prints the BNG box from docklands/data/area.js meta.extent: E 532400-539900, N 176700-182300); "east" = 0.015, 51.495 to 0.085, 51.522 (Royal Docks, ExCeL, City Airport). Items outside both are dropped and counted.
  - TfL lines: a status is kept when an affected stop lies in the zone; TfL sends lat/lon 0, so stops are placed through NaPTAN (stop-area id 940G/910G/930G -> station entry 9400/9100/9300, else by station name); a status naming no stops is kept only for a line that serves the zone (line-wide). Validity periods kept; start = first from, end = last to.
  - TfL buses: route disruptions grouped by identical text; kept when the text names a stop that lies in the zone on one of its routes, else when its opening "STREET, DISTRICT:" is a district wholly in the zone (E1, E1W, E14, E16, EC3*, SE8, SE10, SE16); a borough name alone does not place a diversion. Stop closures placed by the NaPTAN position of their ATCO code, grouped by text and dates.
  - TfL roads: disruptions active in the 90-day window whose TfL point lies in the zone; closed street segments of the same disruption id attached (at most 12).
  - Street Manager: unzip -p of the monthly archive split on }{"event_reference": (record count must equal the zip file count); BNG prefilter on the first coordinate, then OSTN15 to WGS84 and the zone test; the latest event per permit or activity reference. Permits kept when not cancelled, refused, revoked or closed and the end (actual, else proposed) is on or after the fetch date. Activities: skips and scaffolding counted, not kept; cancelled and ended dropped.
  - Gazette: robots.txt Crawl-delay 10 s between requests; only data.json searches and /notice/{id} pages (data.jsonld/.ttl/.rdf/.xml and ?view=linked-data are disallowed). The search geo point is the publisher office, so a notice is kept by issuing authority (zone councils, or Transport for London naming a zone borough). TTRO = text says temporary, section 14, 16A or experimental. Dates = day-month-year phrases on or after publication. Submitter (f:name, f:familyName) and signatory names dropped.
  - Tower Hamlets licences: the weekly table; premises-name column dropped (it sometimes holds a private licence holder name); placed at the postcode centre (postcodes.io, ONSPD). One week per snapshot; history is the git history of the snapshot.
  - Planning London Datahub: description has "temporary" and an event word (event, marquee, festival, stage, market, fair, funfair, ice rink, pop-up, filming, concert, exhibition); centroid in the zone; applications validated more than two years before the fetch dropped; description cut to 220 characters; facts and link only (GLA terms not confirmed as OGL).
  - Markets: OSM amenity=marketplace in the zone from the local extract (ways at the mean of their nodes); Tower Hamlets markets page parsed for name, days, place and times and joined by name, else placed on the OSM street it names.
  - Venue events: the feeds are read from feeds/events.json at run time (category ical, rss-atom, rest-api or whats-on-page; verified; a feed URL; ics, rss, atom or json; id, name or subject about events or what is on; not news, press releases, consultations, meetings, warnings, closures, works or notices; harvest: true/false on an entry overrides), so feeds added there join automatically. Kept per event: title (cut to 160), dates, venue, position, link; no description text. Date from the feed event fields (ICS DTSTART, RSS ev:startdate, WordPress ACF dates), a date in the title or link, or the Atom published day where checked by hand (Royal Docks, midnight entries only); events from today to 180 days ahead; undated items only from single-venue programmes. Position: postcode in the item, else the venue of the feed host (VENUES table, through its postcode). The Royal Docks feed mixes articles with events: only /whats-on/ links.
  - Gazette office postcodes (SE18 6HQ, SE6 4RU, E16 2QU, SE1 2QH, SE1 8NJ, SE1P 5LX, EC2V 7HH, EC2P 2EJ, E1 0HJ, E1 1BJ) are removed before postcode districts are read.
  - Network: User-Agent glitchcan-cwplans/0.1; one request at a time per host, at least 1 s apart (10 s for www.thegazette.co.uk); up to 5 tries with Retry-After or a doubling pause on 429, 5xx, timeouts and resets. Raw responses in data/raw/works/ (gitignored); NaPTAN, Street Manager archives and Gazette notice pages reused unless --refresh; --no-fetch rebuilds from the raw files.
- Inputs:
  - [docklands/data/area.js](docklands/data/area.js)
  - **tfl-unified-api** (Transport for London Unified API: line status by date range, bus route and stop disruptions, road disruptions and closed street segments, TfL open data terms (Transport Data Service; OGL-based, attribution required); the terms page refuses scripted requests: re-check before production): https://api.tfl.gov.uk/Line/{ids}/Status/{from}/to/{to}?detail=true; every line of modes tube, dlr, elizabeth-line, overground, river-bus, cable-car (Line/Mode/...) plus c2c, southeastern, southern, thameslink, greater-anglia; 12 ids per request; from = fetch date 00:00, to = +90 days
  - **tfl-unified-api** (Transport for London Unified API: line status by date range, bus route and stop disruptions, road disruptions and closed street segments, TfL open data terms (Transport Data Service; OGL-based, attribution required); the terms page refuses scripted requests: re-check before production): https://api.tfl.gov.uk/Line/Mode/bus/Status?detail=true; all bus routes with disruptions (13 MB)
  - **tfl-unified-api** (Transport for London Unified API: line status by date range, bus route and stop disruptions, road disruptions and closed street segments, TfL open data terms (Transport Data Service; OGL-based, attribution required); the terms page refuses scripted requests: re-check before production): https://api.tfl.gov.uk/StopPoint/Mode/bus/Disruption?includeRouteBlockedStops=true; all bus stop disruptions
  - **tfl-unified-api** (Transport for London Unified API: line status by date range, bus route and stop disruptions, road disruptions and closed street segments, TfL open data terms (Transport Data Service; OGL-based, attribution required); the terms page refuses scripted requests: re-check before production): https://api.tfl.gov.uk/Road/all/Disruption and /Road/all/Street/Disruption; stripContent=true, startDate = fetch date, endDate = +90 days
  - **naptan** (NaPTAN access nodes, OGL v3.0): https://naptan.api.dft.gov.uk/v1/access-nodes; atcoAreaCodes=490 (bus) and 910,930,940 (stations), dataFormat=csv; cached
  - **dft-street-manager** (DfT Street Manager open data: monthly archive of permit and highway-activity notifications, OGL v3.0): https://opendata.manage-roadworks.service.gov.uk/{permit,activity}/YYYY/MM.zip; the last full month (default); about 1 GB and 13 MB; cached
  - **the-gazette** (The Gazette: Road Traffic Acts, OGL v3.0): https://www.thegazette.co.uk/all-notices/notice/data.json and https://www.thegazette.co.uk/notice/{id}; noticetypes 1501 and 1503 x text = each zone authority, start-publish-date = fetch date - 120 days, 100 per page; then each notice page (cached)
  - **th-licensing-notices** (London Borough of Tower Hamlets: licence applications received this week, council website terms (no open licence stated): facts (type, address, dates, market days and times) and links only): https://www.towerhamlets.gov.uk/lgnl/business/licences/alcohol_and_entertainment/Licence_applications_received_this_week.aspx and .../markets/markets_in_tower_hamlets.aspx; HTML GET
  - **postcodes-io** (postcodes.io bulk postcode lookup, OGL v3.0 (data); service MIT): https://api.postcodes.io/postcodes; bulk POST, 100 postcodes per request

- Faults: 

#### 56. `walk-london-datastore` (tools/walk-london-datastore.mjs)

- Command: `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=1 node cwplans/tools/walk-london-datastore.mjs walk [--details] [--refresh]; NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=1 node cwplans/tools/walk-london-datastore.mjs refs; NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=1 node cwplans/tools/walk-london-datastore.mjs refs-old-wards; NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=1 node cwplans/tools/walk-london-datastore.mjs probe [id ...]; node cwplans/tools/walk-london-datastore.mjs triage; NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=1 node cwplans/tools/walk-london-datastore.mjs harvest [key ...] [--refresh]`
- Method: Walks the London Datastore (a DataPress site with a CKAN-compatible API): reads the whole catalogue from the v3 export, writes it compact (one dataset per line); triages every dataset by written rules (licence class, relevance to the zone, kind, whether the project already holds or lists it, value themes, join keys, sensitivity, score); harvests the shortlisted open datasets and keeps the features that meet the 3D model box, as GeoJSON in WGS84 with a meta member (source, URLs, fetch date, licence, attribution, method, counts).
- Rules:
  - Catalogue: publisher = organisation (logo title), else CKAN maintainer, else custom.author; geo = custom.geo (spatial granularity); update_frequency = custom.update_frequency; resource format = file extension; resource URL = https://data.london.gov.uk/download/{dataset}/{id}/{file}. Not kept: descriptions, contact names and emails, logos.
  - Licence class: OGL v2/v3 -> ogl; Creative Commons Attribution -> cc-by; ODC Attribution -> odc-by; Public Domain, PDDL -> public-domain (these four are open); CC BY-SA and ODbL -> share-alike (never harvested, CLAUDE.md); CC Non-Commercial and All Rights Reserved -> restricted; TfL Transport Data Service Licence -> other; empty -> none (never harvested).
  - Relevance, first match: zone-place (title or tag names a place in the 3D model box; "upon Thames", "Thames Estuary", "Thames Gateway", "Thamesmead" removed first); zone-borough (names only Tower Hamlets, Southwark, Lewisham, Greenwich, Newham, City of London); other-area (names 1-3 places, none in the zone); london-fine (geo finer than a borough, or with no geo a ward/LSOA/MSOA/OA/postcode/UPRN/TOID word or a spatial format); london-borough; london-coarse; unknown.
  - Have: a dataset id or slug in a committed file of cwplans: data-register.json, pipeline.json, tools, data files -> held; a .md note, feeds catalogues, the 2026-10-03 survey -> listed; an "excluded" list or a survey "left out" line -> excluded-before; else new. feeds/london-datastore/ and the generated docs are not read.
  - Sensitive (catalogued, score 0, never harvested): title names homicide, victims, strip or intimate searches, suicide, custody, stop and search, hate crime, domestic abuse, sexual offences, safeguarding, missing persons, rough sleeping (CHAIN), bariatric incidents.
  - Score = relevance weight (zone-place 5, zone-borough 4, london-fine 3, london-borough 1.5, unknown 1, london-coarse 0.3, other-area 0) x max(theme value, 0.5) x recency (newest resource year >= 2024: 1, >= 2021: 0.8, >= 2016: 0.5, else 0.3) x (machine-readable format ? 1 : 0.4) x (a join key finer than a borough ? 1.3 : 1) x (open ? 1 : 0) x (held 0, listed 0.8, new 1).
  - Harvest zone: a feature is kept when its geometry meets the 3D model box (BNG E 532400-539900, N 176700-182300): a vertex inside, an edge crossing (Liang-Barsky) or the box centre inside a polygon. Whole geometries kept, not cut; whole_in_zone and in_cw (meets the Canary Wharf registry box -0.03, 51.498, -0.005, 51.51) are added.
  - Coordinates: GeoPackage SRS 27700, or a custom definition on Airy 1830 with the BNG projection (srs_id 100000) -> BNG; output WGS84 to 6 decimal places through OSTN15. CSV points: a row is WGS84 when |x| <= 180 and |y| <= 90, else BNG; empty or zero coordinates -> no_geometry.
  - Dropped fields: names matching e-mail, phone, contact, fax, mobile, owner name, person (none matched in the 13 harvested files). uprn_suspect: a UPRN field with an exponent or 9+ digits ending in 5+ zeros (F22). The Brownfield Register is read from its CSV, not the GPKG (F23).
  - Network: one request at a time over the whole tool, at least 1.1 s apart; up to 6 tries with Retry-After or a doubling pause on 429, 5xx and network errors; User-Agent glitchcan-cwplans/0.1. robots.txt disallows only /debug, /manage/, /login, /logout.
  - Details walk (--details, 2026-10-04, all 1,305 datasets): /api/v3/dataset/<id> adds per resource the publisher's generic format (kind) and an md5 hash, and per dataset the description; the catalogue keeps a details member (resource count, kinds, archivedAt, nextReviewDate, geo, the granularity words and zone place names found in the description, resources only in the details record). The description text itself is not kept. Result: 0 archived, 0 resources missing from the export, geo identical to the export for all 1,305.
  - Zone references (refs): a code is a zone code when a live or terminated ONSPD (August 2026) postcode whose grid reference lies in the 3D model box carries it, or when its polygon in the Datastore's Statistical GIS Boundary Files (20od9: LSOA/MSOA 2021, OA/LSOA/MSOA/wards 2011, wards 2014 and 2018) meets the box. Zone UPRNs: OS Open UPRN X/Y in the box; zone TOIDs: the LIDS TopographicArea TOIDs of those UPRNs (raw cache only).
  - Area from the data (probe): every open, not sensitive dataset whose metadata relevance is unknown, london-borough or london-coarse (671) is opened: up to 3 readable resources (finer-area file names first, then csv/txt/json/xml, sheets, gpkg, zip; newest first; stop at the first zone signal). Caps: text formats the first 1 MB (HTTP Range); xlsx/xlsm/xls/ods whole up to 20 MB (SheetJS, 3,000 rows and 40 sheets read); zip whole up to 60 MB (shapefile record envelopes and dbf, inner csv/sheets/gpkg, 12 entries, zone borough and place names first); GeoPackage whole up to 100 MB (every geometry envelope). Documents (pdf, docx, images, audio) are not opened.
  - Area class from the data, first match: D1 zone-point (a row's lat/lon or easting/northing, or a feature envelope, in or meeting the box; the value decides WGS84 or BNG); D2 zone-uprn (a zone UPRN in a UPRN column) or zone-postcode (3+ distinct zone postcodes; the postcode's letters must be one of the 121 UK areas); D3 zone-code (an OA/LSOA/MSOA/ward code in zone-codes.json); D4 zone-place (2+ distinct zone place names, 3+ cells); D5 london-fine (5+ London coordinates, small-area codes, 20+ postcodes, UPRN or TOID columns, no zone value in the sample); D6 borough-rows (zone borough code or name, nothing finer); D7 london-coarse (London, region, national, or other boroughs only); D8 none. The data class replaces the metadata relevance of a probed dataset.
  - Final state, first match (every dataset gets one): F1 sensitive; F2 harvested (held); F3 not-open (licence); F3b deferred (excluded before by the project); F4 links only (unavailable when every link failed the Datastore QA check, else deferred); F5 unavailable (every probe request failed); F6 listed-for-harvest (relevant with a machine-readable resource; reason and size); F7 deferred (relevant, documents only); F8 not-relevant (data: borough rows, London or coarser, none); F9 not-relevant (metadata names only places outside the zone); F10 deferred (data not readable: size cap, format, no readable resource); F11 deferred (not probed).
  - Old ward codes (refs-old-wards, 2026-10-04): the 2003 CAS and 2005 statistical ward codes (00BGGG form, ONSPD wdcas03cd and wdstl05cd) of the postcodes in the box, one ONSPD query with distinct values; and the 2011 Census merged wards (E36) of the zone 2011 wards from the ONS lookup WD11_CMWD11_LAD11_EW_LU. Without them 2001- and 2011-Census ward tables read as having no zone rows.
